# Gaps

The work list: what is known to be missing from a production-ready download
manager and is both wanted and possible for free. A row says what is missing
and how it closes; when it closes, its row is deleted (what was done is in
`CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/parity.md` and git history).

| Status       | Meaning                                                      |
|--------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| OPEN         | Nothing done, or done but never run                          |
| PARTIAL      | Works and is tested, but not everywhere it must              |
| BLOCKED      | Needs something this project cannot get for free, or a person |

## How to read this file (people and the free-model bots)

**A row** is `| ID | the gap | its state | how it closes |`: four cells (a `|` inside `code` is
not a cell's edge). The ID is a letter and a number (`K3`); the gap is what is missing, in
a sentence; the state starts with OPEN, PARTIAL or BLOCKED (or "Coded, waits for step 8":
written, not yet run on Windows); the last cell is what is done ("Done:") and what is left
("Left:"). Rows are in the maintainer's order: the sections nearer the top first.

**Markers** in the state cell:

| Marker | Means |
|---|---|
| `· bot` | a free-model bot may take it (`scripts/ai-improve-pick.py` picks the next) |
| `· person` | needs a person: the maintainer's word, a device or a bench, a workflow, vendored code, `unsafe`, or security-critical code |
| `· split` | broken into steps, the rows under it; take a step, not this row |
| `· after K3a` | waits until row K3a is gone (a done row is deleted) |
| `S`, `M`, `L` | small, medium, large |

**A step** (`K3a`: its row's ID and a letter) is one small change in one place, and says:
"Do:" what to change; "Where:" the files (and the functions in them); "Test:" the test to
add, its name and file, and what it shows; "Done when:" the test passes and so does
`cargo xtask test --profile ci`; "Skill:" the pages to read first (`docs/ai-skills/`).

**To take one:** a bot is given its step with its context (`scripts/ai-context.py`). A
person runs `python3 scripts/ai-improve-pick.py --all` for what the bots will take, and
`gh issue list --label bot:improve` for what they have taken, and leaves those to them.

**To close one:** delete its row (git keeps it); if it was the last step of a `· split`
row, bring that row up to date or delete it too. A step that is only partly done is left,
with what is done added to it. Add a `CHANGELOG.md` line in a user's words when a user would
notice. `python3 scripts/gaps-check.py` says whether the file keeps this shape (CI runs it).

**To add one:** write it as a step is written (small, one place, its files, its test, its
"Done when"), mark it `· bot` or `· person`, and a bot can take it the same day.

## Every session first: are the bots working? (the maintainer, 2026-11-24)

The free-model bots keep dm unattended (`docs/testing.md`, "The bots"; the section "Taken
by the free-model bots" below). At the start of every session, before any row:

```sh
python3 scripts/ai-health.py      # reads GitHub only; exit 1 when a check FAILs
```

Each line is OK, WARN (worth a look; the bots still work) or FAIL (they are stopped or broken).
**A FAIL is this session's first task**, before any row here: fix it, then run the check again.

| FAIL | What to do |
|---|---|
| bot workflows not active | `gh workflow enable "AI worker"` (and each named). `disabled_inactivity`: GitHub's 60-day rule; see that the worker writes `.github/keepalive` |
| the worker has not succeeded in 5 hours | Read its last run's log; fix `scripts/ai-worker.sh`; `gh workflow run ai-worker.yml` |
| the guardrail job failed on main | `python3 scripts/ai-skills-check.py` and `bash scripts/ai-selftest.sh` say which guardrail or skill page broke; fix, push |
| AI fix / AI tests / AI merge / AI triage: its last two runs failed | The pipeline, not the model: the failed step of the run named says why (a workflow, a script, a runner change, a CLI's new version). Fix it, run `bash scripts/ai-selftest.sh`, push, start the workflow again |
| no AI fix and no AI tests run in 26 hours | The queue is stuck: `gh workflow run ai-worker.yml` and read its log; `python3 scripts/ai-improve-pick.py` should name a row |
| bot pull requests open over a day | `gh workflow run ai-merge.yml -f pr=N` and read its log: the checks, the review, or `scripts/ai-merge.sh` |

A WARN that stays a week is a FAIL: no bot pull request merged in seven days (read the last
runs' reports on their issues: which check refuses them most; mark small rows `· bot`); no
lesson learned in seven days; no free model answering (`python3 scripts/ai-ask.py pick kilo`,
and `GEMINI_API_KEY` still valid at aistudio.google.com: `gh secret set GEMINI_API_KEY`); a
`bot:needs-maintainer` pull request (read it; merge or close). A session that changes the
bots edits only what the check named, keeps their guardrails (`CLAUDE.md`, "The bots in
GitHub Actions"), runs `bash scripts/ai-selftest.sh` before pushing, and says what it fixed
in `docs/decisions.md`.

## Taken by the free-model bots (the maintainer, 2026-11-24)

The bots in GitHub Actions (`docs/testing.md`, "The bots") take rows from this list by
themselves when nothing more urgent waits: `scripts/ai-improve-pick.py` picks the next OPEN or
PARTIAL row, in this file's order, that needs nothing only a person can give, and
`ai-worker.yml` opens an issue for it (label `bot:improve`, title "Improvement: gaps row …"),
three in flight at most, one new every six hours at most, none again within a fortnight.
Each becomes a pull request that merges itself once CI, Desktop and Android pass on Linux,
Windows and macOS and a review finds nothing; the row here is brought up to date in it.

**Rows broken into steps (2026-11-24).** A large row is marked `· split` and the bots take its
steps instead: rows under it with the row's id and a letter (K3a, K3b, …), each one small
change in one place, saying what to do ("Do"), the files ("Where"), the test to write
("Test") and when it is done ("Done when"). A step marked `· bot` is the bots'; `· person`
needs a person (the maintainer's word, a device, a bench, a workflow, vendored code, `unsafe`
or security-critical code); `· after K3a` waits until K3a's row is gone, as a done step's row
is deleted. **The order they take them in:** `python3 scripts/ai-improve-pick.py --all` (it changes
as steps are done; none is listed here, so nothing goes stale). To add a row the
bots can take, write it the same way: small, with its files, its test and its "Done when".

**Not theirs:** a row that waits for the maintainer (a "decide", their word or approval to
come), or needs a real phone, the Mac, the Windows laptop, a USB disk, the bench or a
measurement on it, a store, a certificate or signing, a screenshot, a change to CI, a
workflow or the release job, or code written on the Mac and not yet compiled on Windows.
The E rows wait for the maintainer, so the bots start at K.

**Steering them:** write `· person` in a row's state to keep it from the bots, `· bot` to
hand it to them first. Before taking a row by hand, see whether a bot has it:
`gh issue list --label bot:improve` (an open issue for the row means a bot is on it).

**Also theirs:** main's red runs (#57, the Windows-only test failures, tried again a week
after each attempt), the site watch's reports (#56), a person's bug with enough to act on,
a feature with three thumbs-up, and each night tests for the file with the most lines no
test runs (#58 is their log).

## Highest priority: the ui-skills review (rows H39, E45 to E51, the maintainer, 2026-11-25)

The maintainer, 2026-11-25: look for interface improvements with the skills in `ibelick/ui-skills`
(MIT, read at commit 7ef0076f), put them at the highest priority, and look at security before
the skills. These rows come before every other row in this file, the E rows below included.
Take them in this order: H39 first, then E45 onward.

**Security, checked before the skills were read:** the repository was cloned into a scratch
folder, outside dm, and nothing in it was run. Its ten `SKILL.md` files hold no hidden or
direction-changing characters and nothing that tells a model to fetch or run anything, apart
from two commands: `npx ui-skills` and `npx @google/design.md`. Those commands, and the remote
MCP registry the repository offers (`https://www.ui-skills.com/mcp`), are third-party code and
text that can change after it is checked. None of them was installed or connected, and none
will be (the clean-room rule). The rules below were taken as ideas only, and none of the skills'
text was copied in.

**What was found.** dm's window was checked against `baseline-ui`, `fixing-accessibility` and
`fixing-motion-performance`, and each finding was proved the way `improve-ui` asks: by the rule,
the code path it reaches and a single fix. Already held, so not rows: motion follows the system's
"reduce motion" setting (`window::follow_motion`); the pointer's animations are 100 to 150 ms
and only fade (`table/cells.rs`); no animation repeats; every phone layer stays inside the
safe area (`window/safe.rs`); numbers use even (tabular) figures; destructive actions use
`native::destructive` and a question; the sidebar, Advanced and the progress window's sections
already say whether they are open (`aria_expanded`). The skills' web-only rules (Tailwind,
React, `h-dvh`, metadata) do not apply to GPUI.

| #   | Gap | Status | How it closes |
|-----|-----|--------|---------------|
| E47 | A pop-up saying whether its choices are open is never read off a tree | PARTIAL · S · person | Done 2026-11-25: `.aria_expanded(open)` on the pop-up button, open while its sheet shows (`PICKING`); a desktop pop-up's choices are the system's menu, which the system announces. Left: read off the system's tree by `human-qa.py` or the emulator (GPUI's test windows build none, so the row's window test cannot be written) |

## The order to take everything in (the maintainer, 2026-11-23)

The maintainer, 2026-11-23: most of the time goes-on testing; the extension should only catch
links and the app do the rest; the phone and the desktop should share one window and one engine,
so a thing is built once; and dm should be faster. So the groups below are in the order that
makes the next one cheaper, not by section. A row stays in its own table, where its detail is.
Found by reading this file and counting lines, not by running anything.

**How a row is tested (the work order of 2026-11-17, kept):** while a group is coded, a row runs
`cargo check`, clippy on its crates and its own tests only. `cargo test --workspace`,
`human-qa.py`, the benchmark and the site checks run once, at the end of the group, and by CI
where group 1 has made that possible.

1. **Tests stop costing time.** A few days, and every later row is cheaper for it.
   - 216: CI on main is red, so no machine can do the testing for a person.
   - 221 and "Watch" below: the wall-clock tests allow for the runner's speed in one way, as
     `bench/` does, so a run is not repeated for a few per cent.
   - 213 (the window's tests abort on Windows) and X8 (clippy for Android).
   - R5: the contract's rows and the benchmark run in CI after a push.
   - 189: a build with symbols, so a profiler names dm's functions (groups 3 and 5 need it).
   - This file (no row): 320 KB, read at the start of every session. The rows are shortened
     as section C's were; a row "Coded, waits for step 8" that the Mac's pass of 2026-11-21
     passed says only what Windows or Linux still owe.
2. **Security that stops real harm, then its proof.** H's steps 1 and 2, in that section's
   order. A row of H that adds a line to the window (H1, H3 to H6, H19, H20, H25, H27, H28) is
   coded when its word is given; if group 4 is not done by then, it is built for both layouts.
3. **The engine's speed** (dm-core alone, so nothing here is redone by group 4). The bench on
   Windows with the GPUI build first (176, 175); then S6 on Windows; 175's two leftovers in the
   engine (the small blocks kept after the start, tokio's second copy of a write); 177 with S5;
   171; S4, 185 and 186 measured; 172; C0's latency.
4. **One window code for phone and desktop.** Before any row that adds or changes a window, so
   each is built once: V8, V9, V10, V11, V12 and V17; N23 and N24; the components V18
   to V25 as a screen needs them; V13. Then V26 and V15, so layout is checked by a machine and
   not by eye. Then one sitting at a phone for all that must be seen there: N31, V14, V21 and
   the rows N that "Mobile first" still lists. The words (V1 to V7) wait for the maintainer;
   given in time, a dialog's words change in the same commit as its layout. Rows E (the
   desktop's design, after rows V) ride on the same parts: E1 and E2 first, then each row as
   its part is built, so a window is laid out once and not again for its rank.
5. **The window's speed** (after group 4: V10 makes the list one code, and a saving made in
   the table or the cards before that is made twice). C0c, 181, S11, S16, S20, the Download
   complete window's 5 MiB (175), 210, 194, 187; then C17's table in the README.
6. **The extension only catches.** A23, before C14, R4 and any other row that touches the
   extension.
7. **What is left of security:** H's steps 3 and 4, and H7, H8, H18, H23 and H26.
8. **Features, each built once:** rows C; the rows W, Y, F and U that are coded or approved;
   then those still waiting, as each answer comes.
9. **Structure and docs:** rows A, X, P and D, a row that shortens later work first (A16, A14,
   A7, A8).
10. **The whole pass on Windows and Linux, once:** step 8 of "Work order" below, the sites
    (Y26) and the long runs (W3, W4).

Rows E stand ahead of all ten groups (the section above). Where this departs from what stands
below: group 1 comes before the security rows, as a red CI
and tests that fail by chance slow those too; nothing else is put ahead of H's steps 1 and 2.
"Mobile first" keeps its place for what blocks an APK; its layout rows are group 4.

Left: a fourth network's, with K3d |

## Next priority: security (rows H, the maintainer, 2026-11-23)

The maintainer, 2026-11-23: "give highest priority to security". Later the same day rows E were
put first ("give it first priority"), so rows H come next: before every other row but rows E
and, since 2026-11-24, rows K, before
the Android rows N, rows C below, and rows V and W. Take them in the order that section gives:

1. A day each, stops real harm: H1, H2, H12, H14, H16, H17, H21, H22.
2. The proof: H10, H11, H13, H15, H29, H30 (H24 done 2026-11-25).
3. What no other manager has together: H3 to H6, H19, H20, H23, H25, H27.
4. The sandboxes: H9, H31.

What does not change: a row marked "the maintainer's word" or "decide" still waits for that
word before it is coded (`docs/behaviour-contract.md`); while one waits, the next H row is
taken, and only when no H row can move does work go on to rows N, then C. A security defect
found by running something (a failing check, a reproduced bug) becomes an H row and goes to the
top of step 1.

## Second priority: improvements from reference C (rows C)

Features from another free, GPL, pure-Rust download manager (reference C) that dm should match
or beat, set as first priority by the maintainer on 2026-11-23 and placed after security (rows
H) the same day; among themselves, the Android rows N still come before rows C. Ideas only, never its code; the
clean-room rule applies. Out of scope: what dm already has, what earlier decisions dropped
(debrid accounts, phone remote control, hosting files, galleries through a helper program), and
technology-only items (encrypted DNS, themes, crash reports). Decisions: `docs/decisions.md`,
2026-11-23.

## Release 0.10.3 (published 2026-11-23)

`desktop-v0.10.3` (Latest), `android-v0.10.3` and the extension's `v3.5.0` were published on
2026-11-23 after 18:00 at the maintainer's word, with the README of rows C15 to C19's first pass.
Checked first: the drafts' files against `SHA256SUMS` and `latest.json`, the APKs' certificate
`f8ad2417…` and versionCode 3, `smoke.py` and `human-qa.py` (75 of 76, B76 needs a debug build)
on the arm64 dmg's app, a public magnet on it (first bytes in 8 s, 2 s with the kept DHT
nodes), and 0.10.3 installed over 0.10.2 on the emulator keeping its list. Left:
- The phone (Galaxy M15): the release APK installed over the debug build, the magnet that was
  stuck, a shared link, a force stop and resume, the screen off (the maintainer).
- The unpublished tags `desktop-v0.10.1`, `desktop-v0.10.2`, `android-v0.10.1` and
  `android-v0.10.2` (their drafts are gone): deleting them was refused to this session; the
  maintainer runs `git push origin --delete …` if they should go.
- Issue #56: the site watch with its probe, for Bilibili's sound read from the page (`78ab6f15`).
  Dailymotion does not pass on the runner: row 222.

## Where it stands (2026-11-21)

`main` is pushed and every crate builds from the repository's root. The test pass (step 8 of
the work order below) has run on the Mac. Everything there passed: ten whole `cargo test
--workspace` runs, `human-qa.py` 76 of 76, `chrome-check.py` ten of ten in Edge (109 of 109 with
`DM_CHROME_YOUTUBE=1`), `youtube-check.py` ten of ten, `bench.mjs fast` fifty runs with none
refetched, the whole benchmark (`bench/results/darwin-2026-11-21`), `stress-check.py hidden`,
and `sites-check.py --public` (9 of 12 sites whole; Dailymotion a sign-up wall, Rumble and
Reddit bot checks). Defects found by those runs were fixed: the panel over YouTube's embedded
player (Y12), a silent Instagram reel offered nothing, two window tests' races, the Android CI's
JDK.

What is left of step 8 is on other machines or needs a person:
- **The Mac, by hand:** G10 (`drag-out-check.py` needs this terminal allowed under Privacy &
  Security › Accessibility), U15 (each notification clicked), U16, 187 (`purge` needs a password).
- **The Windows laptop and Linux in Docker:** their lines in step 8 below.
- **The outside world:** step 8's "Sites" line.

## Mobile first: the order to ship the Android app (the maintainer, 2026-11-21)

**Release test, 2026-11-22 (0.10.1's drafts, then 0.10.2).** The drafts' files matched their
checksums; the macOS app passed `smoke.py` and `human-qa.py` 75 of 76 (B76 needs the debug
build's `DM_STATE_FILE`); the release `.deb` in Docker showed the browser rows not run (a line of
`chrome-check.py` needed Python 3.12), B72 failing (its file chosen after Back up dm opened, where
Debian has no chooser) and Download details' folder field drawn twice; all three fixed, the
checkout 76 of 76 on Linux. On the phone (SM-M156B, Android 16) the release APK installed,
took a shared link, resumed after a force stop and after the server went away, and ran with the
screen off, every file byte-exact; Android 16's start broadcast after a force stop put up "dm
has downloads to resume" beside a running dm, fixed. 0.10.2 carries the fixes. Then a magnet
from a torrent site stuck at "Finding peers…" on the phone (a full swarm, a phone app fetched it):
on Android an `https://` tracker's TLS was checked by the system's verifier, which needs a JNI
context, and its failure stopped the DHT and the other trackers too; librqbit's tracker client
now has webpki-roots (`vendor/patches/librqbit.patch`, `dm(roots)`), and public torrents are
announced to `dm_torrent::PUBLIC_TRACKERS` as well (a magnet with dead trackers: peers in 5 s
on the emulator). Then, at the maintainer's word ("fix the gaps in this release"), a
session starts its DHT from the nodes the last one kept (`dht-nodes.txt`) and five routers,
and a downloading session listens, with UPnP on the public network (Windows and macOS may
ask the firewall question): a DHT-only magnet found peers in 5 to 6 s on the Mac and the
emulator. Released as 0.10.3.

The maintainer, 2026-11-21: "give most priority to mobile. it should be ready", and "signing for
desktop is ignored intentionally". The Android rows (N) come before every other row except the security rows H (the maintainer, 2026-11-23: security highest). The goal is
an APK a person installs from the release page, which does what `docs/android.md`'s checklist
says on a real phone. Today it has run on one phone (Samsung SM-M156B, Android 16): downloads,
Stop and Resume, magnets, a shared link, a YouTube share and the background service all worked.
What it lacks is below, in the order to take it: what breaks or blocks a release first, then
what must be seen working, then the maintainer's word, then the release itself. Each step can be
run on the Mac's emulator (API 35 and 37 installed; API 26 and 29 images to fetch) where no phone
is plugged in.

**1. Fix first: it breaks, or nothing can be shipped without it** (each S)
1. (N19 closed 2026-11-22: ten reopens from the notification on the phone, no crash.)
2. N13: CI's emulator job. 2026-11-22: green on API 35 (Vulkan) on a push. On API 29 dm starts
   but draws nothing: the emulator's GPU there is OpenGL ES 3.0, and wgpu refuses GPUI's sprite
   pipeline (`PipelineLayout with 'poly_sprites_layout' label is invalid`, run 44980900326),
   GLES 3.0 having no storage buffers; the goldfish Vulkan driver is listed but not taken.
   The emulator's own log says why: "Please update the emulator to one that supports the
   feature(s): Vulkan" (Android 10's image has no guest Vulkan). So that job checks dm's start
   and a shared download, and not the window (`DM_NO_GPU`); Android 15's checks all. Left: both
   green on a push; docs/android.md states the least GPU (Vulkan, or OpenGL ES 3.1).
3. (N17 closed 2026-11-22: `android.yml` runs clippy for the phone with `-D warnings`, that one
   lint allowed on Android alone; clean on the Mac.)
4. N14: the Gradle wrapper's jar and scripts (done 2026-11-22: made by Gradle 8.14.3, the jar
   checked against Gradle's SHA-256, `./gradlew assembleDebug` built), then the release job's
   first run at an `android-v<version>` tag. The signing secrets are set (2026-11-21), so the APK it drafts is
   signed with dm's own key.

**2. Must be seen working before it is sent** (a run on a phone or the emulator, then code where
the run fails; smallest first)
5. (N11 closed 2026-11-22 on the phone: a sign-in sealed by a TEE key, read back after a restart.)
6. N12: the exact-alarm permission asked for once, with its reason, and the alarm on time (seen
   2026-11-22); left: a resume after a reboot seen.
7. (N5 closed 2026-11-22 on the phone: the clipboard offer, and a link shared while dm is open.)
8. N9: a torrent's files published when its seeding stops; Export and Back up published on
   Android 10 and up; Remove with file, and the missing-file check, on Android 10.
9. N8: a magnet still finding its metadata follows a change of network; seeding on mobile data
   as N10's lines say.
10. N2 and N3: the CI job's first green run (with step 2), and the APK on API 26, 29 and 35.

**3. The maintainer's word** (asked once, together)
11. N7: the narrow layout's rows (1) to (12), the phone's rows P01 to P12 (`docs/android.md`) and
    the desktop window's least width of 360: the phone's section of the contract, and its QA
    script (N13).
12. N10: the three phone-only lines in Options (Wi-Fi only, Not while roaming, Pause when Data
    Saver is on), their status words and their translations.
13. N8's change on the desktop too: a laptop that changes network announces its torrents at
    once; Wait on a metered connection or a low battery also pauses sharing (B76).

**4. Ship**
14. The release APK at an `android-v` tag: a draft on GitHub (never marked latest), installed
    from it on the emulators and a phone, `docs/android.md`'s checklist run whole.
15. F-Droid: the recipe is in `docs/android.md`; the submission is the maintainer's.

**The same as the desktop, the phone's way (rows N20 to N24 and N31; the audit of
2026-11-22).** The phone runs the desktop's own window, so what it lacks is what the narrow
layout cannot reach and what is shown and does nothing; a desktop habit the phone does its own
way is hidden there, not copied (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-11-22). (N21, web pages, and N22, Open with…, fixed 2026-11-22 and seen on a phone.) N20 (one list of actions, `window/reach.rs`, and its test `every_action_is_reached_by_touch`)
closed 2026-11-23; N23 and N24 finish on it; then N31.

**Later, not in the way of a first release:** N9's folder of the person's own through the
storage picker (SAF, L); N16 moved to GPUI's own Android platform when Zed ships one (Periodic);
Google Play (a fee: BLOCKED, as R1 and R4).

**Release 0.10.1 (the maintainer, 2026-11-22: "i want to release new one today"):** app
0.10.1, extension 3.5.0 and the first Android app 0.10.1, each a draft on GitHub as before.
Done on the Mac: versions raised, CHANGELOG headed; `cargo test --workspace`, clippy with

  160 MB segment).
- `shutdown.rs` `quitting_with_twenty_downloads_running_stops_them_within_two_seconds_and_they_resume_whole`
  (Windows CI; line 145's failure now prints each row's state at the deadline: read it before
  changing the 2 s).
- `limits.rs` `connections_a_server_turns_away_step_aside_at_once` (macOS CI, 1.73 s of 1.5) and
  `a_mirror_that_limits_connections_and_requests_is_fetched_faster_than_one_connection` (Linux CI
  and the laptop, 5.65 to 5.69 s of 5.6).
- `window_tests::lists::the_toolbar_acts_on_the_selected_row` (macOS CI).
- `grow.rs` `a_server_that_shares_one_rate_keeps_eight` (macOS CI, 11 connections of 9).
- `secretstore::credentials`' 32-byte key test (Windows, a whole run).
- `instance::tests::a_dm_starting_while_the_host_probes_waits_the_probe_out` (Windows, under load).
- `human-qa.py` on macOS, late in the walk, 2026-11-23 with 16 connections: B73's "The same file is
  already here" not shown in 40 s (the engine had stopped the row as the same file), and in the
  next run B75's "Followed" window not opened from the side panel; each passed alone three times
  and in the other runs, and the run after was 76 of 76.
- `handles.rs` `toasts_give_their_handles_back` (Windows CI, the release job of desktop-v0.10.2,
  2026-11-22: 46 handles kept of the 45 allowed after 90 toasts; passed on main the same commit).

These wall-clock checks fail on a loaded runner by a few per cent. Next, if they keep failing:
one way for them to allow for the runner's speed, as `bench/` scales its floors by the
computer's measured ceiling, rather than loosening each.

## Bottom line (the maintainer, 2026-11-10)

What every change is measured against. These two never close, and nothing below may be closed
in a way that goes against them:

1. **Better than reference A in every benchmark, in every parameter, on every system.** The
   benchmark is in [`bench/`](../bench/README.md) (`idle`, `fast`, `per_conn`, `flaky`,
   `outage`, `batch`, `big40`, and any case added later). Reference A's numbers of 2026-11-09
   and 10 are frozen in `bench/reference-a-baseline.json` as the floor. It never changes: a
   test and `bench/compare.mjs` pin its digest (maintainer, 2026-11-11). Every parameter is
   judged: time from the address and from the first request, the finish after the last byte,
   bytes fetched twice, memory, CPU and recovery after the outage. dm's median must beat
   reference A's best run, with every file intact. On macOS and Linux dm runs the same harness
   alone, and the machine-bound floors are scaled by the computer's measured ceiling
   (`bench/README.md`). A row closes only when `node bench/compare.mjs` says "better", not just
   close. Any parameter where dm is worse is a row in this list.
2. **Easier to use than reference A.** For the same task dm takes fewer steps, uses clearer
   words and has no dead ends. A gap found here is a row that proposes the change. A change to
   a behaviour in `docs/behaviour-contract.md` still needs the maintainer's approval in words
   before it is made (`CLAUDE.md`).

What reference A does comes from four folders of local notes. They are listed in
`docs/engine-local/sources.md`, which is git-ignored because the notes name it. Their findings
reach dm only as black-box items in `docs/engine-local/requirements.md`, and a run of both apps
must prove each one.

## Work order (the maintainer, 2026-11-17: the coding first and fast, the tests last and at once)

Taken after "Mobile first" above (the maintainer, 2026-11-21). The order the rows below are taken in; the rows themselves stay in their tables, where their
detail is.

**While coding (steps 1 to 7):** a row is coded with `cargo check`, clippy on the crates it
touches and its own tests only (`cargo test -p <crate> <name>`), then committed. The whole
`cargo test --workspace`, `human-qa.py`, the benchmark, the site checks and every "owed on
Windows / Linux / by hand" are not run row by row: they are step 8, once. A row whose code is
done moves to "Coded, waits for step 8" in its Status and is deleted only when step 8 passes it.
A row is not held up waiting for an answer: the questions are asked together (above), and coding
goes on with the rows that need none.

**Before step 1: the GPUI migration closed.** Checked on the system each names: G11 (Mica drawn
black on Windows 11), fixed and seen; 213 (`dm-gpui`'s test process ending with 0xc0000409 on
Windows); 211 seen (Light and Dark at start and from Options), then `human-qa.py` whole on
Windows (G7); U43 (a drag on each of the list's bars, then the two window tests ported); G10 (a
finished file dragged out, seen landing on each system); G6 (IME by hand; on Linux the 50-window
churn and the package's size); G8 (the release workflow's packages from a draft through
`human-qa.py` on every system).

| 181 | (optimization spec §3.2; [audit](optimization-audit.md#32-progress-coalescing)) A wake of the main window still reads more than what changed, and builds the whole window again when anything did. Done (2026-11-12, macOS): `SavedSizes::apply` is linear (a set of the finished rows, not a search of every row for each; a unit test of 10,000 finished rows holds a read under 100 ms in a debug build, where it took seconds) and gives a finished row the same copy while the queue's row is the same, so an unchanged read hands out the same pointers; `Downloads::read` compares the rows by pointer and, where only figures moved (`placed_alike`), keeps the names, places and choice, working them out again where a row was added, removed or changed otherwise, after Options, and every 2 s while anything changes (`NAMES_EVERY`), and sorts again only where it may matter; the names' folder cache (`names.rs` `FREE`) drops what expired rather than emptying itself past 2,000, which at 10,000 waiting rows asked the disk for every row at every read; a wake draws only where the read found a change, a request was answered, something shown moves with the clock, or the wake changed what stands beside the rows (`needs_drawing`, with a unit test); the window's frames are counted and read on the port's own thread (`ui.frames`), and `ui-bench.py` reports `idle_frames`: 0 at 10,000 rows over 30 s (it woke and drew every 30 s); the wake channel holds at most one wake of each kind (`dm-gpui/src/wakes.rs`, with a test). Measured in the runs that closed row 180 (`ui-bench.py --rows 10000 --runs 2 --idle 30 --only idle,list,busy`, release, macOS, 2026-11-12, the other track building and benchmarking): CPU with 20 downloads at 10,000 rows 39.6% to 24.9% (the merged build's four redraws a second had taken it from the stored baseline's 26.4%), idle CPU 1.3% to 1.1%, one scroll 0.028 s (the spec's 50 ms met); `stress-check.py window` peak CPU 31% of a core (the audit: 61%). Done too (2026-11-12, later, macOS): the queues are worked out from the rows where the list changed (`queue_list`: Start and Stop Queue's arrows, the menu bar, the side panel), no longer asked of the engine at each read and again at each tick, a pass over every row under the queue's lock each; and while only figures change, the rows on screen and those chosen are named again every 2 s (and at the next read after a scroll), not every row: at 10,000 waiting rows that was 5,000 `lstat` calls a second on the window's thread. Profiled first with macOS's `sample` on a `profiling` build (10,000 waiting rows, 20 downloads queued at 1 MB/s): the window thread's reads were half its work, the naming 180 samples of 580 and the two queue passes 157. Measured (Apple M1, release, `ui-bench.py --rows 100,10000 --only busy`, 3 runs each, nothing else running; the base build measured before and after the new one): CPU with 20 downloads at 10,000 rows 17.8% (16.5 to 19.7; 19.3 and 19.5 in two runs before) to 13.9% (13.5 to 13.9), better than the audit's §9 (17.2%); at 100 rows 13.4% to 12.8% (11.3 to 14.5). At 10,000 rows (`--only idle,list`, 2 runs) idle CPU 0.5%, no frame drawn idle, a scroll 0.023 s, paging 0.028 s (worst 0.033), a sort 0.044 s (slowest 0.051), the filter 0.086 s, Find next 0.014 s, emptied 0.033 s; `stress-check.py window` (5,060 rows, 60 downloads) answers in a median of 5 ms, worst 65 ms, peak CPU 26% (the audit: 43 ms, 127 ms, 61%). Coded too (2026-11-18, Windows, by reading and writing only: not built or run, the maintainer's word that this laptop builds no more; to be built and run on the Mac): what a read changed is found once, by pointer, in the session's read (`snapshot::Changes`: the rows the session held from the last read compared with the new, the places that hold another entry, or every row where rows came, went or moved; `Read::changes`), and each pass follows only those rows: the detector (`Detector::read_changes`; the read before kept as what changed, `before(id)`, not a second map of every row), When done's watch (`DryWatch::follow` keeps the busy rows, `look_moved` judges from the rows whose state moved, the same answer as `look`), the events (`Events::publish_changes`), `going.json`'s running rows (`Publisher` follows their places), the finished rows' sizes (`SavedSizes::apply_at`, the rows whose file was missing still looked for at each read, as before), and the window (`refresh.rs` `read_rows`: only the changed rows are taken into its rows, compared, and counted for `moves_with_time`; sorted again only where a changed row compares otherwise under the sort). The wake is `Downloads::wake`, and the window counts its builds (`Downloads::renders`). Tests written, not run: `session/wake_tests.rs` (10,000 rows, 4 changed, a read beyond the snapshot under 5 ms in debug; a read of the changed rows tells what a read of every row tells, step by step), `snapshot/tests.rs` (`Changes` by pointer; `apply_at`; 10,000 finished rows, 4 changed, under 2 ms), and the window's `window_tests/status.rs` `a_wake_that_changed_nothing_draws_nothing_and_a_changed_row_draws_once` (a test that counts renders). Not measured before or after on this laptop (the build was stopped before the first measurement). Coded too (2026-11-20, Windows, by reading and writing only, not compiled: the laptop codes only): a row changed in place in more than its figures (a download started, stopped or finished, a row renamed or sent to another queue) no longer has every row named and placed again (`window/list/placing.rs` `restate`): that row is named again, and, where its naming moved (settled, placed, renamed, another folder), the rows not yet placed of its old or new name and folder and those whose count of earlier rows of their name moved (`names::earlier_and_alike`, one pass); it alone is placed again (`Places::place_at`); the queues are worked out again only where its queue changed, the files taken by a later row only where a finished row changed; and the list is filtered and sorted again only where it came into or left the place shown, or the sort may move it. The tick's own looks follow the reads: the rows running, paused or failed are kept by id from what changed (`Known::busy`), and the taskbar's mode, the clock's interval, the heap's give-back, the speeds sampled and the hints look at those alone; "Delete file after" is looked for only while a row has a time (`Known::deleting`); the application's tick goes through the rows only for a read that finished or failed one (`session.rs` `told`). Tests written, not run: `places/tests.rs` `placing_the_changed_rows_alone_counts_as_placing_every_row`, `names/tests.rs` `the_rows_alike_are_found_beside_the_counts_in_one_pass`, `placing.rs` `only_a_row_settled_placed_or_renamed_moves_the_others_names`. Not coded, and why: views of their own with `.cached()` for the parts that did not change. The words a script reads and clicks (`Screen`, `ui.texts`, `ui.click`) are recorded as each frame is prepainted, which a cached view skips, so a cached part would drop out of `ui.texts` and fail `human-qa.py`; caching only while no port records would leave the cached window unchecked by QA; and a cached part is built again only when told (`notify`), so each needs a key of everything it shows, or it stays stale on screen. To be measured first (what the unchanged parts cost a frame, `ui-bench.py --rows 10000` on a `profiling` build), then decided with the maintainer. Still owed: build, test, clippy and `cargo xtask privacy`/`layers` on the Mac, then step 8 (`ui-bench.py --rows 10000`, `stress-check.py window`, CPU with 20 downloads and a download finishing at 10,000 rows, before and after); the parts of the window that did not change are still built again with the rest (above); the schedules' tick (`schedule_tick`: `looked_after`, `end_of_window`) still goes over every row at every tick. | 185 | (optimization spec §5, §5.2; [audit](optimization-audit.md#52-download-manager-safeguards)) CPU and disk work has no bounded pool and no limit of its own, and Cancel cannot stop it. The runtime is tokio's default (`dm-desktop-base/src/boot.rs:101`): one worker per core, and up to 512 blocking threads shared by every file write, assembly and copy. A digest runs on the async workers between its reads (`dm-core/src/checksum.rs:109-127`). Each ZIP extraction gets an OS thread of its own (`dm-shell/src/app/files.rs:154`), and so does each JavaScript evaluation (`dm-core/src/javascript.rs:434`). Assemblies, digests and extractions have no limit apart from the network's connections. After Cancel, an assembly copy (`dm-core/src/download/assemble.rs:43`) or a digest still runs to its end. A slow disk should slow the network read and not grow memory (the write is awaited before the next read, with 16 KiB per connection), but that is by design only, and untested. Done (2026-11-12, macOS): digests, joins of parts, MP3 and GIF making and ZIP extraction take turns, as many at once as half the cores (at least one), set apart from the network's limits (`dm_core::heavy`: a plain counting lock with a bell, so an extraction's own thread waits for a turn as work on the runtime does, there holding no thread). A digest reads and hashes on the blocking pool, not on the runtime's workers between reads, and looks at its download's control every 256 KiB; a join copies 32 MiB at a time (`io::copy` of a `take`, the kernel's copy kept on Linux) and looks between. Only a Cancel stops them: a pause leaves them to finish, as a join or digest cut short would lose the joined file a resume needs. Tests: a 2 GiB sparse file's digest ends within 1 s of a Cancel and not under a pause (`checksum.rs`); a join of 8 parts of 256 MiB ends within 1 s of a Cancel and leaves nothing under the name (`download/mod.rs`); `tests/slow_disk.rs` holds the parts' writes to 1 MB/s (`slow_part_writes_to`, a test hook) under 8 connections from a server sending 50 MB/s: 6.3 MB written in 6.1 s, the process 4.7 MB larger at most (within 10 MB). The blocking pool keeps tokio's 512 threads on purpose, said in `boot.rs` (the writes are one per connection, awaited; the heavy work waits outside it). Coded 2026-11-20, not compiled: one part copied to another drive (the path row 166 measured worth having), which ran to its end after a Cancel, now stops part-way too (`download/assemble/across.rs` `copy_across`). On Windows it is still the system's own copy, `CopyFileEx` called as `std::fs::copy` calls it (a name past `MAX_PATH` in its `\\?\` form), with a progress routine that looks at the download's control as the copy begins and after each block and cancels the copy on a Cancel (the system removes the file it had begun); elsewhere it is a piece of 32 MiB at a time, as the join of several parts copies (`copy_pieces`, now shared by both: on Linux the kernel's copy, as `std::fs::copy` was; on macOS a read and write through 4 MiB where `fcopyfile` was, a clone being impossible across drives). The part is removed only once the copy is whole, as before; a rename on one drive is unchanged. Left: JavaScript's evaluation (Windows' engine, a thread each, 2 to 3 s at most) takes no turn, as a video's start waits on it; `node bench/bench.mjs fast per_conn big40` (3, 3 and 2 runs, release, macOS), `compare.mjs`: every parameter better than the floor but memory, as before (row 175: 116, 119 and 122 MiB); big40's CPU beside the base build, run straight after it, 1,880 and 1,990 ms against 2,530 and 2,150 (the computer's hour moves it more than that: 1,430 earlier the same night). Linux: owed to the final Linux pass (the three tests; Windows the same on its laptop) | PARTIAL · coded 2026-11-20, not compiled (the laptop codes only): the copy across drives stopped by a Cancel, with a test written, not run: `download::assemble::across::tests` (the copy held to its span; a Cancel at the second look ends it with `Cancelled`, the file short of the whole or gone, the part kept). The Mac runs `cargo test -p dm-core --lib download::assemble`, `cargo clippy -p dm-core --all-targets`, `cargo xtask privacy` and `layers`; Windows (CI or the laptop) runs the same test, the only build of `CopyFileEx`'s routine, and times a 512 MiB file saved to the hard disk D: against row 166's copy (1.4 to 3.2 s) | Digests, extraction, assembly and other CPU work run on a bounded pool whose size is set apart from the network's limits. The blocking pool is sized on purpose. Cancel stops a digest or an assembly of a 2 GB file within 1 s (a test). A test with the parts' writes slowed to 1 MB/s, under 8 connections offering 50 MB/s, shows resident memory flat (within 10 MB) and the download slowed. `node bench/bench.mjs fast per_conn big40`, then `compare.mjs`: no worse |
| 186 | (optimization spec §5, §8 scenario 12, §9; [audit](optimization-audit.md#5-async-runtime-and-threads)) Quitting while downloads run now stops them and waits for them (2026-11-12, macOS): Exit, a logout and SIGTERM, in the GPUI window, the egui window and `--headless`, call `Backend::shut_down` → `Queue::shut_down` (`dm-core/src/queue/actions.rs`), which starts nothing more, stops each running download as a pause stops it (each range writes what it holds and its ledger), awaits every run up to 2 s (`SHUT_DOWN_WITHIN`), aborts any still going, puts the rows back as Queued and writes the queue. `dm-core/tests/shutdown.rs`: 20 downloads of 2 MiB on 2 connections each, paced at about 400 KB/s a connection, stopped part-way: the shutdown took under 2 s with no run left (3 runs, each whole test 3.9 to 4.0 s), every row came back Queued, and the next start finished all 20 byte for byte. `stress-check.py quit` (new): SIGTERM to the GPUI window's release build with 10 of 20 downloads running (dm runs 10 at most), 22 MB fetched: exit 0 after 0.10 s; the restart kept 24 MB, ran them again and finished all 20 byte-exact. Done too (2026-11-12, macOS): a range stopped by a pause (a person's, or dm ending) first takes and writes what has already come and is waiting to be read, without waiting for more (up to 512 KiB a connection, `DRAIN_MOST`), then syncs its file and writes beside its ledger that those bytes are on the disk itself (`done-NNNN.synced`, `stopped_synced`); the next run trusts a range up to that mark without asking the server for its last 64 KiB again (`RESUME_CHECK` stays for a ledger with no mark: a crash, a power cut). A mark is forgotten wherever a ledger is cut back, and goes with the parts. In `tests/shutdown.rs` the bytes fetched twice went from 2,981,120 (about 73 KiB a connection) to 0.78 to 1.47 MB in five runs (19 to 36 KiB), the restart itself fetching 30 to 460 KB of it; the test now holds it to 48 KiB a connection. Still owed: the row's 16 KiB. What is left is what the server had handed its side of the connection, and dm had not been given, when dm let go: the test counts bytes as its server yields them to the connection, which takes in its own send buffer and the pieces yielded before it sees the connection closed, neither of which reached dm. Measuring what dm itself received twice (the server counting what was acknowledged, or dm counting what it read and dropped) is the next step before deciding whether anything of dm's is left. Linux: owed to the final Linux pass (the test, and a SIGTERM to `dm-gpui` under load). Windows, coded 2026-11-18 (not yet built or run; the maintainer moved testing to the Mac): Windows ends a session (a logoff, a shutdown or restart, an installer's Restart Manager) by sending `WM_QUERYENDSESSION`/`WM_ENDSESSION` to top-level windows, and may end the process as soon as they answer; a console's logoff and shutdown events never reach a program with user32 loaded. GPUI's own hidden window took `WM_ENDSESSION` as a quit and called `exit(0)` straight after its quit handlers, with none of dm's registered, so the downloads were cut off mid-write; now the window registers `on_app_quit`, which does what Exit does before the window goes (`wind_down`: `Backend::shut_down`, the port closed and forgotten, the frame kept) unless Exit already did. And dm keeps a hidden, never-shown tool window of its own on its own thread (`dm-desktop-base` `signals/session_end.rs`, class `dm-session-end`, started from `dm-gpui` `main.rs` for the window and for `--headless`, which has no GPUI window): `WM_QUERYENDSESSION` is answered yes, and on `WM_ENDSESSION` (true) it calls `Backend::shut_down` and closes the port before it answers, then sets `SIGNALLED` and wakes dm, whose own Exit then finds nothing running; a session that was not ended after all changes nothing. Ctrl+Break (`CTRL_BREAK_EVENT`, what a process manager sends a console program it stops) now ends dm as Ctrl+C does. Console close was already right: tokio holds its handler until dm's main returns. Tests written, not run: `dm-desktop-base` `signals::session_end::tests` (the hidden window is not visible, answers `WM_QUERYENDSESSION` with 1, does nothing for `WM_ENDSESSION` false, and answers `WM_ENDSESSION` true only after the ending has run); `stress-check.py quit` on Windows ends the window's dm by the session's end (`end_session`: each top-level window of the process asked and told, as Windows does) and checks exit 0 within 2 s, then ends the restarted `--headless` dm the same way and checks the same, and a third start finishes all 20 byte-exact. Still owed on Windows: run those, and `cargo test -p dm-core --test shutdown` on this laptop (only Windows CI has run it); the 16 KiB measurement needs dm to count what it read and dropped, a counter in `ranged.rs`'s read loop. Coded 2026-11-20, not compiled: each range's attempt counts what it takes off its answer (`Reading::received`, with what it was handed to write first) and, at its end whatever the end, what of it it did not write, less what it reads on into the range after it (`download/unwritten.rs`, `left_unwritten`): the 64 bytes before a range set aside for its seam's check, what came past a range's end and was dropped, a chunk in hand when the attempt stopped; a resume's tail compared counts what agreed (`tail_checked`). The seams' own checks (`assemble::seams`) are not counted. The total since dm started is `dm_core::read_not_written()` (a test hook, as `slow_part_writes_to` is), and each attempt that counts any writes `range N: B bytes read and not written` to the finish trace (`DM_FINISH_TRACE`). `tests/shutdown.rs` prints it beside the server's count, before the restart and by it, and holds it to nothing yet | PARTIAL · coded 2026-11-20, not compiled (the laptop codes only): the counter, with unit tests written, not run (`download::unwritten::tests`). The Mac runs `cargo test -p dm-core --lib download::unwritten`, `cargo test -p dm-core --test shutdown -- --nocapture` three times (the line `dm read and did not write … a connection`, from which the row's 16 KiB is decided), the ranged tests that read the loop (`--test seam_check --test limits --test speed --test halving`), `cargo clippy -p dm-core --all-targets`, `cargo xtask privacy` and `layers` | On Exit or SIGTERM with 20 downloads transferring, each one is stopped as a pause stops it (buffers flushed, ledgers written). Its task is awaited within a deadline of 2 s, and dm exits 0 within that time. A test measures the exit time (a `stress-check.py` phase, or one in `dm-core`). After a restart, the rows come back as they do today, every file finishes byte-exact, and no connection fetches more than one buffer (16 KiB) twice |

## The audit of 2026-11-11 (rows P, Y, S, U, F, A, D, R)

Asked for by the maintainer on 2026-11-11: "look for improvement … what things to be added or
remove to make it better app … like [reference A] but clean room implementation. No third party
plugin or package", "over all UI UX improvement", "website like youtube download and how to make
it better", "optimize the code and make it faster", and "architecture and maintainable code …
highly optimize and maintainable structure even noob can follow … pure rust and GPUI". Found by
reading the code and the docs; each row's Status says how far it has come.

Two goals these rows add to the bottom line, in the maintainer's words of that day:

3. **Clean room, pure Rust, GPUI the only window toolkit on desktop (Compose on Android, row N1, 2026-11-20), no helper program.** Nothing fetched
   and run, nothing found on the computer and run in dm's place. Rows P.
4. **A beginner can follow and maintain it.** Rows A and D.

"the maintainer's word (Bxx)" marks a row that changes that
contract row, which is edited in the same commit as the change (`CLAUDE.md`). "decide" marks a
row where the first step is a decision recorded in `docs/decisions.md`; the recommended option is
named. Size: S a day or less, M days, L weeks, XL more. Rows in each table are in the order they
are worth doing.

### P. Clean room: helper programs, third-party feature code, a second toolkit

| #   | Gap | Status | How it closes |
|-----|-----|--------|---------------|

### S. Speed, memory and CPU (root causes for rows 171 and 175 to 177)

The Windows numbers in rows 175 to 177 are the egui build's; the only GPUI data is macOS's
(`bench/results/darwin-2026-11-11/`). So the first act on Windows is `node bench/bench.mjs` with
the GPUI release build. Tried and dropped already, not to be proposed again (`docs/decisions.md`):
another allocator, HTTP/2 for speed, 256 KiB write buffers, full preallocation, `panic = "abort"`,
staging on the download drive, copying to it during the download.

**The order (2026-11-23, from the rows' own figures; nothing run that day).** Where dm stands:
download time leads or ties reference C in six of its seven scenarios, latency left (C0); CPU is
under reference A's floor in every scenario on macOS and over it on Windows in `idle`,
`per_conn`, `flaky` and `big40` (176); CPU on a slow server is 3 to 6 times reference C's (C0c);
memory on macOS is 107 MiB at rest and 117 to 133 downloading against floors near 21 (175); the
first frame comes at 370 to 410 ms with 10,000 rows (S16). So, after rows H and N:

1. C0c: the window redraws only when what it shows changes. The largest gap; measure the
   window's share against `--headless` on `capped` first.
2. `node bench/bench.mjs` and `compare.mjs` on Windows with the GPUI release build: 175's
   Windows memory is still the egui window's, and 176's CPU there is from 2026-11-13.
3. S6 on Windows only, kept if `cpuMs` falls by a tenth.
4. Memory that is dm's own: 175's three leftovers on macOS (the 5.7 MB of small blocks the start
   frees and the heap keeps, tokio's second copy of each write, the Download complete window's
   5 MiB), then S20.
5. S16: compile and measure the first screenful's naming, then the window's own 190 ms and the
   shaders compiled at each launch.
6. 189 before S11: a release build with symbols, so `sample` names dm's functions at 10,000 rows.

### F. Features against reference A, and beyond it

Present and checked, so not rows: splitting and reuse, queues and the Scheduler, the recheck
queue, the download allowance, the speed limiter with slower hours, dial-up, the program run on
each file, ZIP contents and Take out, Update download link, site sign-ins, proxies with SOCKS and
PAC, FTP and FTPS, MMS, RTSP and RTMP, numbered series, the clipboard, the take and leave keys,
site and type lists, form answers, Digest and NTLM, the parts folder, start at login.

### A. Architecture a beginner can follow

What is already good and stays: the crates form one line with no cycle, held by `cargo xtask
layers`; `dm-protocol` is small, versioned and shared cleanly with the extension; `unwrap`,
`expect` and `panic` are refused by clippy in every shipped crate; every `unsafe` block must say
why; every file sampled opens with what it is for.

| #   | Gap | Status | How it closes |
|-----|-----|--------|---------------|
| A4  | Names no longer say what things are: the shipped binary is `dm-gpui` while `--help` says `dm-desktop` and the .deb installs a shell wrapper under the old name; `dm-shell` is the application layer, and "shell" also means the window. One word, several layers: scheduler in four crates, Add URL in three, `probe` (ask a server; the control port's answers), `control` (the stop flag; the TCP server), `progress` three times, `rules` four. ## Best in its market (rows W, the maintainer, 2026-11-17)

Asked for by the maintainer on 2026-11-17: "things we need to implement to make the application
best in the market of this category … better than [reference A] and best in market which user
wants". Found by reading this list, `docs/feature-status.md`,
`docs/parity.md`, `docs/feature-matrix.md` and a search of the source for each thing, not by
running anything; each row's Status says how far it has come.

A fifth goal beside the four above, in the maintainer's words of that day:

5. **Best in its market, and what a person wants.** Ahead of every download manager a person can
   get, free or paid, not reference A alone, and holding what people ask a download manager for.

Every row that adds a control, a dialog, a line of Options or a
word to the window changes what `docs/behaviour-contract.md` fixes or adds to it, so it is marked
"the maintainer's word" and waits for approval in words before it is coded (`CLAUDE.md`). "decide"
and the sizes are as in the audit of 2026-11-11.

**What already puts dm ahead, and must not be lost** (each proved in `docs/parity.md`): free with
no reminder to pay; Windows, macOS and Linux where reference A is Windows alone; nothing phones
home; YouTube, the muxer, MP3 and GIF with no helper program; BitTorrent; a checksum file read by
itself; Metalink and mirrors; a control port and MCP; a headless run; secrets sealed in the
system's store.

**What stands between dm and "better than reference A" today** is already in this list, and comes
before every row W: memory (175), CPU on Windows (176), the finish after the last byte on Windows
(177), `flaky` and `outage` on Windows (171), the `batch` case (172), the site list's 59 "not yet"
lines (Y26), Reddit's sound (205), nothing signed (R1), no store listing for Chrome-family
browsers (R4), an update that only opens its installer (F6).

## Android (rows N, the maintainer, 2026-11-20)

The maintainer, 2026-11-20: "we will make it android app too. all feature should work like
desktop. we can share the link in the our download manager and it will downlaod and it will
work like a bittorrent android app". Found by reading
the tree at c010a3dc; each row's Status says how far it has come (first run on a phone 2026-11-21).

**What is meant:** dm on Android phones and tablets with the desktop's features (segmented HTTP
with resume, the queue and its schedule, YouTube and the streams, BitTorrent with magnets and
`.torrent` files, Options, the categories), started by sharing a link to dm from any app (the
system's share sheet) or by opening a magnet or `.torrent` with it, and downloading on in the
background with its progress in a notification, the way a phone's BitTorrent apps do.

**What carries over and what does not:** the engine crates hold no window code (`dm-core`,
`dm-net`, `dm-media`, `dm-torrent`, `dm-protocol`, `dm-js`'s Rust side) and are expected to build
for `aarch64-linux-android` once their system code is given an Android branch (A10's
`platform/` is where it goes). YouTube's player checks need no system engine on the phone: dm-js is
dm's own interpreter in Rust (row F15), so row N4 (a WebView's JavaScript) was dropped on
2026-11-20, found by reading `dm-js/src/lib.rs`. The window does not carry over: GPUI has no Android backend, so
the phone needs a window of its own, and goal 3 above ("GPUI the only window toolkit") cannot
hold for it as written. dm-shell's tray, login item, registry, dial-up and the browser
extension's native host have no Android meaning.

**Decided (N1, 2026-11-20, at the maintainer's word, three times):** first Compose over UniFFI,
then Rust with Slint screens, then, the same day: "UI should be share. like we minimize the
desktop it becomes mobile view" and "use GPUI and build the andorid as well". The phone runs
dm-gpui itself, on a GPUI platform for Android that dm writes (N16); the window lays itself out
as a phone's screen when narrow (N7); everything under it is the desktop's (`docs/decisions.md`).
The Kotlin app and the Slint shell were removed unbuilt; the shell's JNI modules are kept.


| #   | Gap | Status | How it closes |
|-----|-----|--------|---------------|
| N16 | GPUI has no Android platform of its makers': its platforms are macOS, Windows and Linux (`gpui_linux`, `gpui_windows`, which dm carries as patched copies) | PARTIAL · XL: 2026-11-20, `vendor/gpui-android` made from gpui-mobile at 5fd54f4554263ae06753e90472c7f2e2ff5d7495, its Android platform alone, by `cargo xtask vendor gpui-android` (GitHub's archive of the commit checked against its SHA-256, `GPUI_ANDROID_REMOVE`, `patches/gpui-android.patch`; the patch checked to make the copy byte for byte from the archive), marks `dm(android-only)` and `dm(port-0312)` with rows in `vendor/README.md`; moved to gpui-pre 0.3.12 (`platform_info`, `on_displays_changed`, `refresh_interval`, `on_display_changed`, `gpu_specs` an `Option`, `RequestFrameOptions`' new fields); `cargo check --target aarch64-linux-android -p gpui-android` clean, no warnings. Java and Gradle as gpui-mobile has them: the maintainer's word, 2026-11-20, "use gpui-mobile." (`GpuiInputActivity`, `GpuiPathPicker` in `crates/dm-android/java`, the Gradle project in `crates/dm-android/android`); no other Java. 2026-11-20, later: dm-gpui's many windows as a stack on the one surface (`dm(android-stack)`, README row; `src/stack.rs` the plain logic, `src/android/layers.rs` the forwarders, `AndroidPlatformWindow` keeping its callbacks in its layer): before, every GPUI window wrapped the same `ANativeWindow` and registered its callbacks over the last window's, so the newest took frames and input and once it closed nothing drew the one below; now the newest shown is drawn over the whole surface whatever size it asked for, frames, touches and keys go to it, size, appearance and visibility to all, active status to the top alone; one closing uncovers the one below, repainted in full; `insets` gives the status and navigation bars as GPUI's safe area (dm-gpui keeps clear of it, coded 2026-11-20, not compiled: `window/safe.rs` pads each window's root by `Window::insets().effective()`, zero on the desktops so nothing moves there; the phone layout's drawer and sheet placed below the status bar and above the navigation bar; a unit test of the padding); Back (on release) is Escape pressed and released in the top window, then, where GPUI says no one used it and the top is unchanged, that window closed as its close button does (`on_should_close` asked), else on the first window `Activity.moveTaskToBack(true)` (downloads go on). dm-gpui, narrow layout only: Escape (`Dismiss`) closes the ⋮ sheet's submenu, the sheet, then the drawer (`narrow_back`); on Android alone an unused `Dismiss` is passed on so Back reaches the platform; a window test `escape_closes_the_sheet_and_the_drawer_one_step_at_a_time`. `patches/gpui-android.patch` written again without the network (the archive rebuilt by reversing the old patch, which it then reproduced byte for byte; the new patch checked to make the copy exactly). Coded, not compiled since the maintainer's word of 2026-11-20 (no more builds on the laptop): no `cargo check`/clippy of gpui-android or dm-gpui after this change, the new window test not run, `cargo xtask layers` not run; only `src/stack.rs`'s 4 tests run (`rustc --test`, pass) and rustfmt. gpui-android is a stand-in: when Zed ships an official GPUI Android platform dm moves to it and removes the copy (the maintainer, 2026-11-20, "when offical come we will shift but for now use gpui-mobile"; Periodic below). ## Safer than any other download manager (rows H, the maintainer, 2026-11-23)

Asked for by the maintainer on 2026-11-23: what can be done on Android and the desktop to make
dm "much better secure" and better than the others in its market. Ideas only, nothing coded.
Found by reading the source, the manifest, the workflows and both security reports, not by
running anything, so each row's first step is to confirm the gap. A row marked "the maintainer's
word" adds a line, a dialog or wording to the window and waits for approval; "decide" is as in
the audit of 2026-11-11.

**Already there, not to be redone:** secrets sealed in the system's store (the phone's TEE key);
the update manifest and `SHA256SUMS` signed (F6, W21); the mark of the web and macOS's
quarantine on finished files; checksum files read by themselves; redirects, playlists and
Metalink held off inside addresses, DNS rebinding caught at the connection; unpack limits
(size ratio, nothing outside the folder); a peer blocklist and peer encryption for torrents;
`cargo audit` and `cargo deny` in CI; workflow actions pinned by commit; `allowBackup="false"`
and only the needed components exported on the phone; `SECURITY.md`.

### H, first part: the file a person is about to open (both systems)

| #   | Gap | Status | Next |
|-----|-----|--------|------|

Left: vendor tracker-comms for a body cap and a least interval, a cap on a peer's outstanding requests, Split into H33b–H33e (below), which change vendored code and its patches, so a person's. The cap and the dropped peers are tested since 2026-11-25 (`a_peer_sending_endless_pex_cannot_grow_the_known_peers_past_the_cap`, a made-up seeder that hands over real metadata, then PEX). |

Mark it `dm(caps)`, update `vendor/README.md`, write `vendor/patches/librqbit.patch` again. Vendored code: a person's. Left: the row says "not encrypted" for an `http://` download; a checksum that came unencrypted is called "matches, but could have been changed on the way" |

## Shorter words, and one window that fits phone and desktop (rows V, the maintainer, 2026-11-23)

Asked for by the maintainer on 2026-11-23: text that is short and professional, a better
layout, and one UI code in GPUI that adapts to phone and desktop. Ideas only, nothing coded.
Found by reading `dm-gpui`, `assets/i18n/template/catalog.json` and `docs/ui-design.md`, not by
running anything. Every row here changes wording or layout that `docs/behaviour-contract.md`
fixes, so each waits for the maintainer's word, changes its contract rows and
`docs/behaviour-baseline/` in the same commit, and ends with `human-qa.py`.

**Where it stands:** the window is already one code for both (`dm-gpui`), with a second layout
below 600 points (`window/narrow.rs`). Three things hold it back:

- The choice between the two layouts is made by hand: `is_narrow` is asked about 70 times in
  20 files outside `window/narrow/`, which is itself 1,562 lines. A screen is in effect written
  twice.
- There is one breakpoint, so a tablet, a phone on its side and a half-width desktop window
  get a stretched phone or a squeezed desktop.
- The words were written for a wide dialog: of 1,030 strings, 150 are over 60 characters and
  24 over 120 (the longest is 283); capitals are mixed ("Stop All", "Delete Completed" beside
  sentence case).

### V, first part: the words

|-----|-----|--------|------|

Longest first (the dial-up, firewall, "Only through", backup and seeding texts) |
| V5  | The row's status, sizes and dates have one form; a card on a phone needs a shorter one | OPEN · S | One formatter with a full and a short form ("45% · 2.1 MB/s · 3 min", "Yesterday"), picked by the room the text has, never by the system |
| V6  | Nothing keeps the words short once they are fixed, and a translation can be far longer than its English | OPEN · S to M | `cargo xtask words` (beside `i18n`): fails on a string over its kind's limit, on Title Case, on a word outside U20's list, and on a translation over 1.4 times its English. V15's pictures catch what still does not fit |
| V7  | All fourteen languages carry the old long strings | OPEN · M, after V2 to V4 | Translate the new short strings; the changed ones go to each language's unread list (W23) |

## A window read at a glance, on desktop and phone (rows E, the maintainer, 2026-11-23)

The maintainer, 2026-11-23: people who tried dm say the desktop's design is, overall, not good;
make it better and adaptive for phone and desktop; learn from reference C's user experience,
without looking like it. Found by putting dm's own pictures (the README's, taken 2026-11-16, and
`docs/behaviour-baseline/gpui/macos/`) beside reference C's published pictures and help pages of
the same windows. None of its code was read and nothing was run, so a row begins with a fresh
picture (`ui-shot.py`): Options (C21) and the dialogs (N31) have changed since. The feedback
named no window; if those who gave it name one, it becomes a row here.

**What is wrong, in one line:** every part is there, but nothing is ranked. Each toolbar command,
each row state and each line of a dialog has the same weight, so the eye has nowhere to start;
text is cut while the room beside it stands empty; and a form's fields start wherever their
labels end.

**What reference C does well** (how it is used, not how it looks):

- Its progress window is ranked: the status and a whole-file bar first, limits and "when done" a
  click away, each connection's detail behind a Details button.
- A row is read without reading its words: a picture for each file type and a mark for where it
  came from.
- A form is a grid: labels in one column, fields in the next, the file's picture and size beside.
- Help is one click from the dialog it explains, pictured in light and dark with numbered steps.
- Everything can be drawn larger, up to 200%.

**Already held, so not rows:** a look for each system (`window/theme.rs`: Windows, macOS, GNOME,
each with the system's accent); a question's own choices ("Also delete the file", "Remember my
choice", "Don't show this window again"); Open with…; Undo in place of a question (B23); one
search box over name, address and note, where reference C opens a Find dialog.

**Not taken:** its look (a grey classic window, a coloured toolbar, a tree of folders); Options
as two rows of tabs (C21's list of pages is easier and narrows to a phone); tabs in the progress
window (a tab hides what is set, and a phone has no room for a strip); a choice of many themes.

Every row is built in V9's parts, so it is drawn once and the phone gets it; "Compact" in a row
says what a phone then shows. A row that changes a contract row waits for the maintainer's word,
and changes that row and `docs/behaviour-baseline/` in the same commit. Each row's commit shows
its window before and after at 360, 700 and 1280 points wide, light and dark.

## Periodic

- Search GitHub for copies of dm that drop `NOTICE` (`dm-engine`, `com.dm.engine.json`,
  `easy-download-manager@pranavo72bex`) and file a DMCA notice for one. Last run 2026-11-09:
  no copy (the one `dm-engine` crate found, `dionkadi/rDM`, is a separate Tauri and SQLite
  program).
- Look for an official GPUI Android platform (zed-industries/zed); when one ships, move to it
  and remove `vendor/gpui-android` (row N16, the maintainer, 2026-11-20). Last looked
  2026-11-20: none (issue #43206 closed not planned; #12039 open).
